ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-04-28-DSPG106.u88	Thank you. Socialist Parliamentary Group. You have the word to Mrs. Acuña do Campo.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-04-28-DSPG106.u89	Thank you, President. Good morning, ladies; good morning, Counselor. I also want to take this opportunity to greet the professionals and the professionals of diet, diet and nutrition, who are sure to follow this debate from somewhere. Dietists-nutritionists are self-defined as those diet and nutrition experts who develop activities designed to achieve adequate food for physiological or pathological needs, either for a person or for a group of people, taking into account the principles of prevention and public health. It is precisely, ladies and gentlemen, these professionals, dietists and nutritionists, who today demand our support for the creation of the Official College of Dietists-Nutritionists of Galicia through this law that they proposed to the Government of Unity in their day. From the Socialist Parliamentary Group we want to make it very clear that we have no doubt about the usefulness of professionals who, as experts in nutrition, nutrition and diet, have the ability to intervene in people's lives, both in health and in disease. No doubt, ladies and gentlemen, about the usefulness of the dietary council, or about the needs of research and teaching in this matter. There is no doubt that they also intervene through government agencies in public health, as well as in food companies and in collective and individual restoration. But what we have doubts about, ladies and gentlemen, in the Socialist Parliamentary Group, is about the obligation of professional schools, because in practice they are indeed a barrier to those professionals who finish their training and who, even without having real chances of entering the labour market, if they are forced to cope with paying, rates, collegiate quotas, in some very high cases; without forgetting that, unfortunately, and also speaking frankly, some of the professional schools are not working well, no. They lost their status as an organization born to support, to inform, to teach, to support the colleges and colleges and to become a chiringuit more to the service of the people who direct it. Instead of thinking about the interests of the people in college, they only think about what is interesting to the person or persons who preside over them, and even the government who puts them to their service. But, said this, we're going to look at the bill we're dealing with today. Without a doubt, the first one to draw attention is the time elapsed between which it was approved by the Board of Trustees, in 2012, and the entry into this House, in 2015. It's true that reports were requested from different bodies, but it's also true that these were provided at that time. Besides, I don't know why we had to wait three years. Because the truth is that the text that was presented in 2015 is very similar to the one proposed to the Government by that collective as early as 2012. We don't want to think that Mr. Feijóo had done with the request of nutritionists and dietists the same thing he does with almost everything: to wait for the election year. He did so with the health infrastructure, leaving – yes, it seems extremely serious – I will not tire of repeating it – in the drawer the money that Europe gave us to make these infrastructures, years and years, without taking into account, first, the health needs that there was; second, the economic paralysis that caused the crisis; and without taking into account the needs of the unemployed and the stops in this country. He took it to his, and in the drawer until he arrived in 2015, election year; when then he pulled it out and started the infrastructures late, badly and never. Although in this case it may also have influenced, as the vice president of the board also acknowledged, the mess, the tremendous mess that mounted the government of the state with the preliminary draft law of professional schools. So messed up, ladies and gentlemen, that, as with many other things, eventually became paralyzed. And, of course, between trouble and trouble, there was a Constitutional Court ruling in 2013 that supports the collection, and also mandatory, for all health professionals, they exercise in public or in private. Something that, of course, we socialists respect, but we don't share. But what is most appealing to us is not delay, but that the People's Party and the People's Government will support this law based on the fact that it is a health profession. What are we left with, gentlemen? What are we left with? Is nutrition and diet a health profession? Then, how do you understand that Mr. Feijóo is committed to privatizing all the food in public hospitals, he, the same president who assured that he would never privatize the white robes, that in one would privatize anything related to health care? Ladies and gentlemen, the Socialist Parliamentary Group will support the consideration of this bill by voting against the amendment proposed by the Joint Group. The fundamental reason we have for this is that it is the professionals themselves who make this request. Professionals who work daily to improve our health through food and who believe that school creation can improve their work. They claim that academically they are a collective with the necessary training and qualification. By the way, it must be pointed out in this line that the College of Doctors of Ourense intended to include senior technical persons in diet, but this contribution was rejected by the Government. They also argue that the Galician community has the competence to create the Greek professional school of dietists and nutritionists – this school, in fact, has been reflected by the vice president too, already exists in five other autonomous communities – and that it is also a collective that is already united under a umbrella, organized under a umbrella, which is the Galician Association of Dietists and Nutritionists, and that the school would allow them to go a little farther. And they also argue that it will be very useful for them to create the school to fight intrusion, although this is not so clear, ladies and gentlemen, because there are professions, such as physiotherapy or even medicine, which, in spite of the burning official schools, are suffering from intrusion. Of course, school doesn't interfere, but it can't cut down on intrusion or any other problem. Dietists and nutritionists also base their claim on the fact that, when it comes to a health profession, it is subject to the Health Professional Management Act, which includes not only the necessary academic training and the skills and attitudes peculiar to health care, but also that people engaged in this activity are organized in officially known professional schools. This law also considers health professions, people performing health professions, such as those licensed in medicine, pharmacy, nursing, dentistry, physiotherapy, physiology, optics and optometries, dental prosthetics and hygienists, logopeds, nutrition and diet and occupational therapy. However, and as the Competition Council also collects in its report of allegations, the collection contains quite differently in one and another health profession. Some have a state school, others have an autonomic school, others have state and autonomic school, others have provincial schools. For example, medicine has a General Council of Medical Colleges in Spain, and then official schools of provincial doctors. Physiotherapy, physiology and speech therapy have two schools, one state and the other autonomics. The hygienists and dental hygienists don't have state school and they do have a professional school of Greek dental hygienists. And nutrition and diet does not have state school in Spain either. So, if this law were passed, they would only have – as in the case of dental hygienists – an autonomic school. But I wonder what will happen now to occupational therapists. Yes, ladies and gentlemen, they are the only ones on that list of health professions that would be excluded, that would be left without school. It's not easy to understand why some healthcare professions have up to two schools and others, however, they don't have any. Nor is it easy to understand how 50% of the nutrition and diet professionals work in hospitals in Europe, and here Mr Feijóo prefers such questionable catering and flame. The Socialist Parliamentary Group will reject, as we have already said, the amendment of the Joint Group. But, yes, urging that this next school of nutritionists and dietists in Galicia never forgets that it was born to improve the quality of this benefit, and that it cannot be a barrier to those people who, for whatever reason, cannot assume the cost of the rates of compulsory collection. For now, nothing more and thank you very much.

ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-04-28-DSPG106.u100	Good afternoon. Let's restart the session. Opinion of the Economic and Industrial Councillor, on his own request, to report on the Industrial Competitiveness Agenda in Galicia
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-04-28-DSPG106.u101	You have the floor, for your intervention, Mr. Francisco Conde's economic and industrial adviser.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-04-28-DSPG106.u102	Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen. Today I come to this House with the purpose of presenting to you the Plan Director of Industry-Agenda for Competitiveness Galicia Industry 4.0, and I do it with a strong desire to promote constructive and positive dialogue, a dialogue that can overcome political differences and a dialogue that is primarily useful for companies working in Galicia. So, ladies and gentlemen, I hope that on this occasion we can go beyond repeating the slogans and the wordplays of previous plenary, and I really hope to collect your proposals, the opposition proposals, to enrich an agenda that the Greek industry needs to strengthen itself and grow. Because what's at stake is their future, our future, because industry is important yesterday, today and tomorrow. In this government we have made it clear that our bet is precisely to set industrial fabric in Galicia, because it is the sector that has historically shown to create more and more jobs and quality. Ladies and gentlemen, we have already achieved the necessary macroeconomic stability to re-grow and re-create industry and build the pillars of the Greek economy. Because when we say that Galicia meets the target of the deficit, that Galicia controls indebtedness, that keeps the investor effort and that it pays the suppliers swiftly, we're talking about measures that have driven a trend change, a trend change that we've managed to get Galicia out of recession in 2014, after the German GDP is chained four consecutive quarters with positive rates and increased by 0.5% in inter-annual terms; that this trend also starts to have positive effects on employment. With data in March 2015, there are 24,300 unemployed less than a year ago and 17,000 more members of Social Security. That for the first time since the beginning of the crisis, 2008, there's an increase in domestic demand of 1.1%, which gives us an idea that recovery is beginning to come slowly and also to families. Something is happening in the business sector, because gross capital formation, which indicates the evolution of its investment, has again been positive since 2007 too, in this case with an increase of 1.3%. So now is the time for a policy, it is the time for a strategy by and to strengthen the industrial sector and make it the driving force behind the growth of the Greek economy. A plan that collects the Galicia Industry 4.0 Competitiveness Agenda, within the framework of Horizon 2020, on-line with the European Union guidelines and, as we did at the time, with the Strategy of Smart Specialization, the RIS3, and the Strategy of Internationalization of Galicia. So, we're dealing with a roadmap that's optimizing the exogenous and territorial factors that affect competitiveness, and a roadmap that also affects endogenous factors, and that will therefore serve to advance a new production model that is more based on innovation, technology and value creation. And we do it, ladies and gentlemen, with a double objective. Firstly, industry is growing to provide 20% of the German gross domestic product in 2020; and, secondly, create 50,000 new jobs to develop and that Galicia's industrial occupation recovers levels prior to the crisis, in this case in 2007. But before I begin to sketch out the Galicia Industry 4.0 Competitiveness Agenda, let me make a brief diagnosis of the Galician industry today and the changes that have been made in recent years; a realistic diagnosis, an objective diagnosis that draws us what is the tendency of the Galician industry and helps us identify also what their needs are. The economic crisis in recent years has led to a fall in the weight of the industrial sector in gross added value, in this case a fall of 2.8 points, according to the Galego Institute of Statistics, from 15.9 % in 2007 to 13.1 % in 2014. And this is something, ladies and gentlemen, which has been common, unfortunately, in the whole of Spain and also in the whole of the European Union. In particular, in Spain, the gross industrial value added fell 1.8 points, from 15 % to 3.2 %, i.e. one tenth just above Galicia's position, and in the case of the European Union that fall was 1.3 %. Even so, at present, there are 12,676 industrial companies in Galicia, companies that occupy 153,000 people, and are also generating gross added value of more than 9,000 million euros. So, ladies and gentlemen, we have a productive fabric based on small and medium-sized companies, which will be the main objective of the various measures of the Galicia Industry 4.0 Competitiveness Agenda. But we won't forget big companies like Indidex, PSA Peugeot Citroën, Estrella Galicia, Finsa, companies that are especially important for their tractor effect on the economy and their contribution to Galicia's image. Finally – and we have said so too – the recovery of the Greek industry is now more fragile and complex than we would like to be. In fact, the annual IPI rate continues to be negative, -3,5 %, in February this same year. But despite that, too, ladies and gentlemen, we have positive symptoms. Exports since 2013-2014 have exceeded the figures for any previous year. There are 5,876 less unemployed people in the industrial sector, which is 15.4% less than a year ago. The business trust index is 5.8% higher than a year ago. And it also increases the confidence of the Galician families, with an indicator that improved by more than 15 points in the first quarter of the year with respect to the same period of the year 2014. Ladies and gentlemen, the situation in the Galician industry is still due to the effects of the crisis, but it is true that there are a number of structural factors that have always been there and that we must continue. However, it is also true that as we move away and go beyond the year 2007, the Welsh industry is improving its ability to compete, a capacity that it has already proven. The detailed analysis of the favorable and unfavorable factors that condition our industrial fabric is collected in the Agenda, which is accessible on the Igape website, and therefore in this appearance, I will just mention some of the most important aspects in the view of this government. Thus, within the limiting aspects, we would highlight, firstly, high corporate atomization, i.e. the existence in Galicia of a large number of small enterprises that are making it extremely difficult to achieve the productivity of other economies such as Germany, France or Italy. Secondly, there is a clear need for greater diversification and greater technological intensity of our productive tissue. We also need to continue to work on greater cooperation with a strategic vision, especially in the areas of innovation and the development of internationalization. And they also know that in Galicia we have a high concentration of exports in certain markets, mainly in the European Union. On the positive side, we also have important factors. We're talking about a productive fabric that has continually increased companies based on technology and many with international success. We are maintaining the positioning of leading industrial poles in strategic sectors, such as textiles, automotive, health or food, and even with very significant advances in emerging sectors such as the aeronautical sector or the biotechnology sector, which are showing great potential. We also have more modern and competitive logistics and support infrastructures in Galicia, we have clearly identified capacities and growth potentials in the Strategy of Smart Specialization and we have, ladies and gentlemen, new instruments and policies with a focus on industrialization. In this context, the global trend, the trend of the international economy is aimed at reinventing industry through the smart factory; a reality that is developing in international markets and which logically Galicia and Spain also have the responsibility to participate in the reinvention of our productive fabric. And, in this way, the industrial policy of the German Government that I present to you this afternoon seeks, precisely, to adapt our efforts to that reality, always seeking a balance between supporting specific sectors and a set of horizontal actions aimed at completing market action. Because, ladies and gentlemen, they are companies, they are industries that share the same purpose: competition and growth, and they must do so necessarily in a context of growing international competition. We are, ladies and gentlemen, in the face of a real revolution that goes beyond mere automation, and that unites, for the first time, companies, universities and technology centres with the common objective of achieving sustainable and competitive models, models in which robots and humans work together in areas with total connectivity, where cyber systems accelerate productivity and innovation, where manufacturing technology such as the additive production of 3D printers is the protagonist, as are the new materials and advanced industrial services. And all this without forgetting that these products have to get to the market and that therefore they have to meet the demands of increasingly efficient consumers who need an ultra-personalised production, an axial production, which, of course, is also open to small businesses. So, ladies and gentlemen, the Industry 4.0 model is an unquestionable reality, it's a reality that we mustn't or can refuse. Germany, the United States, the United Kingdom and South Korea are already moving forward in their smart factory programs. But change is not limited to them. Other regions very close to Galicia have the attention placed in this model. We're talking about the Basque Country, in the case of Spain, but also about Flanders, Belgium, Rome or the southern Netherlands. Industry 4.0 which is in no way exclusive. It's a new way of addressing industrial processes and a new way of creating value through technology, talent and innovation. In this sense, the smart factory model is for everyone and will prove decisive for both the most advanced and high-tech industries as well as the most traditional and small industries. This is the case, for example, of the company on line Mymuesli, which, with only 18 ingredients, allows its customers to customize their product to more than 566,000 million in different ways. Or we're talking about companies like Molca, which, thanks to the 3D printing, are able to reduce the time and cost of launching new products on the market. Ladies and gentlemen, a change in how to produce, a change in how to compete, which is understood by several companies in Galicia. We're talking about companies like GKN, like Delta Vigo, like Televes, like Coasa, like PSA Peugeot Citroën, that all walk towards greater robotization and greater digitization of their productive systems, and that they do it with the increasingly qualified staff, with a permanent goal of doing things differently and doing things better. Zero inefficiencies, zero defects and the highest quality. Because Industry 4.0 and intelligent manufacturing is much more than robots and machines, gentlemen, much more. I was telling you, ladies and gentlemen, that Industry 4.0 and intelligent manufacturing is much more than robots and machines. [[Speak words that are not perceived.]] Technology is already a commodity that can be taken anywhere in the world. The technology our companies want to compete with can lead to anywhere in the world. All of them, ladies and gentlemen, are playing an important role in the transformation of the manufacturing processes of our companies through automation and robotization solutions. Ladies and gentlemen, Industry 4.0 is definitely not a slogan. This government, of course, must and wants to meet its demands. [[Applause]] In this sense, the Galicia Industry 4.0 Competitiveness Agenda that I present to you is born of the proximity of the Italian entrepreneurship, as well as the proximity of clusters, technological centres and other economic agents present in our country. In total, more than one hundred and twenty agents participated, among whom I want to highlight especially the companies themselves this afternoon. We are talking about large and small companies, advanced or traditional companies, more or less innovative companies, exporting or non-exporting companies. All of them collaborated in the elaboration of this agenda, an agenda, ladies and gentlemen, of industry, an open and inclusive agenda that reflects public-private collaboration to put into practice a new smart industry that can turn a global revolution into a local opportunity. [[Speak words that are not perceived.]] This dialogue started last year led us to design five major impulse programmes in the Galicia Industry 4.0 Competitiveness Agenda, with concrete measures to meet specific needs and objectives. I am referring to the improvement of territorial factors of competitiveness, to more professional organizations and more qualified people, to deepen business capitalization and growth, to drive a component of greater innovation and, finally, to expand markets. Five programmes with specific objectives and measures which I shall briefly summarize. The first program on the Agenda seeks to improve the exogenous and territorial factors. We need, ladies and gentlemen, to adapt the ecosystem to make it easier for the Greek industries to be more dynamic, more competitive and more intensive in the use of new technologies. So commitments are precisely generalizing and intensifying the use of ICT in companies, especially in small and medium-sized enterprises. It also facilitates more economical and better-equipped industrial soil, an action that, as you know, we have already begun to carry out and which is producing important results. Strengthen and coordinate the different logistics infrastructures. And finally, promoting doing business, that is, facilitating business creation through regulatory simplification and axillization in different processes. As you know, we have generally eliminated municipal opening licences, we have already published rules such as the decree to facilitate the opening of new petrol stations through a unified and integrated procedure. And we will continue, ladies and gentlemen, we will continue with measures such as the launch of a business doing office in Galicia, which will address, through an online platform, all the complaints and all the proposals and suggestions that businessmen transfer to us. The objective is clear: that administration is no obstacle to the creation of a business in Galicia at any time. The second program focuses on people and organizations. We're talking about talent, we're talking about technological and organizational excellence. With the Agenda we will seek to connect training systems with the new needs of industry, and we will do so through a firm bet on dual vocational training and higher education. We will facilitate the knowledge of models and technologies for the intelligent factory, and we will do it through identification, demonstration and advice on trends, and also through training on their adaptation and implementation. In parallel, we will all make a new effort to strengthen the Greek industries through their cooperation. And we will also enhance industrial entrepreneurship, with focus on sectors of great potential and the creation of startups. We already have a clear example in the Agrobioteg Innovation program, so the biotechnological, food and biomass companies have a specific support to consolidate and grow, and we will continue with similar initiatives. The third program on the Agenda aims to focus on the growth and size of our small and medium-sized enterprises. The size, ladies and gentlemen, is, without a doubt, one of the great eyves that has the Italian industrial fabric. With the new European funds 2014-2020 we will promote lines of funding that will favour the investments of new industrial establishments or the expansion and improvement of existing ones, without forgetting, of course, the support for companies that still need to adjust their financial structure, because this action will not fail to be a priority for the Greek Government. Therefore, together with the European Funds 2014-2020, which included in-depth grants and innovative financial instruments, XesGalice will continue to offer funding through participatory loans and shareholdings in corporate capital. Igape will continue to make available to pemmes the Jeremiah loans, the first initiative, as you know, which offers our loans with European funds to our pemmes. And the EIB's new loan line will allow EUR 135 million to support the circular, so that pemmes can grow in size and finance new investments. With this objective, we will also promote the diversification of our products and businesses through hybridization of different sectors. Ladies and gentlemen, in Galicia we already have examples of this potential. Examples are the application of advances by the manufacturing industry in the automotive sector, the ICT industry in textiles or shipbuilding in the marine wind. So our challenge will be to put more initiatives into practice. The fourth program refers to innovation. It includes measures to create value and design our intelligent factory model. As you know, the challenge 2 of the Strategy of Smart Specialization focuses precisely on the drive of industrial competitiveness and, therefore, on the development of this model of intelligent manufacturing. We are ultimately driving and favoring innovative projects as a basis for creating the smart factory, without, of course, helping any kind of competitive innovation. We will encourage the transfer of research results to the market – no transfer and no innovation –. And, of course, we will encourage the collection of public and private funds to implement Industry 4.0, mainly through EU funds, such as Horizon 2020. Fifthly, the fifth programme of this Agenda will support the aspects of internationalisation. In this sense, we will try new ways to attract foreign investments that allow our value chains to close here in Galicia. For this reason, the Board of Directors has just approved an injection of nearly 2 million euros designed precisely to encourage foreign investment, an initiative that will be developed in sectors of particular interest to Galicia, such as the agri-food sector, automotive, naval, aeronautics, transport, ICT or logistics. And in addition, we will continue to focus on our commitment for new markets and the diversification of our external sector, where the support of the Pexga Network, in collaboration with the Confederation of Galician Entrepreneurs, will be decisive. So, ladies and gentlemen, we have a strategy in place. We have a plan, ambitious, designed and consensual with our own Welsh industry and with a clear goal of facilitating all possible tools to make our productive fabric more competitive and more sustainable; an agenda to support our companies' adaptation to the keys of the smart factory, without failing to address at all times the needs of companies that don't opt for this model. On this path, the Galicia Industry 4.0 Competitiveness Agenda will look for the strength of each and every one of our productive sectors, with the clear objective that they grow and create jobs in our community. All productive sectors will have measures specific to their needs, some with ad hoc measures and with specific and specific measures. I'm talking to you about the naval sector, for example, where, as you know, we've agreed to encourage the development of innovative new technologies in the naval sector, which will facilitate access by our shipyards and our industry to assist industry 4.0 manufacturing technologies. Even so, it will be key to the more than 11 million euros that we will invest in the development of the aviation sector in Galicia, with a clearly emerging opportunity and in which the aviation sector bet is final. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-04-28-DSPG106.u191	Thank you, Mr. Jorquera. People's Parliamentary Group. Mr. Puy.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-04-28-DSPG106.u192	Good afternoon. Ladies and gentlemen, allow me to say that before we move forward the vote of the People's Parliamentary Group on this motion I have three questions in advance: one, theoretically, from the political economy; one second, legally, from the right; and one third, practically, from the data. Always taking into account – as you said in Commission 3a – that we are all in favour of fighting tax fraud, although we can differ in how to be more effective. Theoretics. Tax fraud depends basically on two issues, the predisposition to fraud and the opportunity for fraud, which depends heavily on recorded economic activities. And these two questions, predisposition and opportunity, are conditioned by different variables. Classics are the cost of infringements, both by the probability of being arrested or perceived as fraud; the probability of being punished; the content of the penalty, or the costs of complying with tax obligations. Logically, pondered by the benefits of fraud, which have a great deal to do with marginal tax pressure. They also have to do with aversion to the risk of taxpayers, but they also have to do with – and modern financial sociology thus demonstrates – issues such as the social stigmatization of fraud, the perception of the equivalence of the tax system, the perception of the fairness of the tax system or the perception that exists in the group about the existence of many or few tax frauds. These are the conditions for tax fraud, and of course this can be concluded that falsely increases the perception that there is a lot of fraud or that there is no fight against fraud is counterproductive, precisely for the purposes of combating tax fraud. The second consideration is legal. Both the Constitution and the Law on urgent measures of the year 1977 – urgent measures of fiscal reform in which sources of Quintana lay the foundation of our tax system – and the Constitutional Court in many decisions clearly states it. E quote or Constitutional Court: "In particular, it recalls the duty of secrecy which weighs on all authorities and officials who have knowledge of data revealed in tax investigations. Please note that it must be strict and complete and that it is especially protected. Of all perhaps the most known is that of Lahur Sisa, a young Hindu. In one of his variants, he tells the legend that a ruler of India to defend his city can defeat the invaders with a great strategy despite having fewer soldiers. Despite this victory, the sadness overwhelms him because in that war-flag he lost his only son, who was the heir prince of his throne. Neither prayer nor gift nor entertainment could alleviate their sadness until Lahur arrived and offered him in audience a new game that he did not know. Explained by the rules, Lahur in one of the games defeats the sovereign and explains that this defeat results in defense of uselessly a bishop, who represents the prince, but that sometimes it is necessary to sacrifice one of these pieces to maintain the position of privilege in a match, or, as had happened in the battle, in order to maintain the peace and freedom of the people. The contented sovereign gets the joy, and on the basis of that joy, he proposes that he choose the gift he likes most. And in the general laughter of the entire court, Lahur asks for a grain of wheat for the first house, twice for the second, twice for the third and so on, to which the monarch explains to his vassals that give him a bag of wheat grains. Lahur reacts and says there's not big enough in the bag or large enough in all the territory of India to pay the debt the sovereign committed himself to. So the palace mathematicians count and the conclusion is that planting the entire territory of India for a century there would not be enough grain to pay off the debt. This legend may be true or may be false, it may be a legend or not, but what if it gives us is the true dimension, the extraordinary dimension that can achieve a game, both from the point of human values and from the point of purely commercialism. The importance of this game, which can be played in itself, of sport, of science, of art... any one of them would be attributable, and I'm sure some more given its importance, as well as pedagogical, which is the one that occupies us here today in this proposal. The European Parliament made a specific declaration on 15 March 2012 for chess in school to be incorporated into the educational systems of the European Union, marking the beneficial effects that chess could have on children of any age, based on scientific evidence, including, for example, creativity, intuition, memory or ease of developing skills and values such as motivation, determination and deportability. The Congress of Deputies, at the initiative of the Socialist Parliamentary Group and with a compromise from the People's Parliamentary Group, also adopted an initiative for the specific implementation of chess practice in schools, also in public spaces and their momentum as sport. The efficiency and relevance of chess as a pedagogical tool in which all initiatives are influenced are based on rigorous and highly varied scientific studies of many nationalities. But I would like to highlight one that, for the closeness that touches me and for the bond, deserves to be reflected in this House. I'm referring to the one developed in 1993, two years before UNESCO had recommended its incorporation into school curricula, to a project developed in a Fene school, headed by the Russian-made at Ferrol Boris Zlotnik, doctor in Pedagogy, former Professor of Chess at the University of Moscow, who has studied with boys and girls aged 7 and 5. Finally, after applying the Raven test, he concluded that 7-year-olds and boys had improved by 65% and 5-year-olds by 25%. These these theses were later confirmed by a study that developed the Galician Union, also here in Santiago, in two schools applying the tests at first, then doing an analysis after a course studying chess at a school and not at another school, and the conclusion was that the level of intellectual coefficient of those who studied chess was far higher than those who had not studied it. We're talking about the Yaque Project, which – as I say – developed into two schools in Santiago. On 11 March, the Councillor for Culture, Education and Universitary Order, present in this House in the debate, signed with Silvio Danailov, President of the ECU, the Union of European Chess, and with the mayor of Roundola an agreement that put on the stage the experience developed in schools of this municipality, to try to incorporate the study of chess into the curriculum. This memorandum, which is part of the Council's line of promoting innovation and combating school failure, is the framework for the methodological basis in which the Galicia ECU project will be organized. This initiative is not unique, it is added to some that are already in progress, such as that in the classes the association is lit Xaquedrum, in collaboration with the Council, which allows to study and develop the activity of chess with materials in the Greek language. We believe that it is the time, given the process in which we are implementing LOMCE, to develop the curriculum and methodological change that allows to deepen and boost the actions undertaken for the application of chess in schools, as a tool and a means of educational improvement. We understand that the reinforcement and presence of chess in the classroom will help to improve the student's key competences, including reading understanding and mathematical calculation, which, by the way, are not very well stopped in international reports. But as important as academic results are aspects related to the formation of the individual as a person. Today to talk about the assessment of consequences, correct decision-making, long-term planning and reformulation of plans before new situations, acceptance of established rules, tolerance to defeat, critical analysis of wrong decisions or learning from mistakes is talking about chess, but it's also about education. Chess facilitates the understanding of other subjects, therefore the transversal character of its implementation, and encourages the extraversion and self-esteem of the individual, and promotes the spirit of overcoming and independent mode of action. There is not a single master in the Welsh educational system who does not use educational games as motivational educational material, and chess, there is no doubt about it, at this point, is a genius element to do it. Ladies and gentlemen, these are the reasons why the People's Parliamentary Group presents this non-law proposal, in a constructive sense, and we look forward to the support of all groups. Nothing else and thank you very much, Mr. President.
